State research dossier · Delaware

Delaware Cannabis & Hemp Research

Last audited: 24 October 2026. This audited edition places the independent primary-sources-only audit first, followed by the complete report (Parts 0–12, all sections kept) with the audit corrections applied in place. Figures and flags still unresolved after the audit are kept flagged in the report and listed at the end of the audit.

Delaware has legal medical marijuana (2011, 16 Del.C. Ch. 49A) and legal adult-use possession (23 Apr 2023, HB 1) with a licensed commercial market under the Delaware Marijuana Control Act, 4 Del.C. Ch. 13 (HB 2, 2023), regulated by the Office of the Marijuana Commissioner (OMC) in the Department of Safety and Homeland Security. Adult-use sales did not start until 1 August 2025, through converted medical compassion centres.

Complete — Parts 0–12 (Runs 1 + 2). All sources accessed 24 October 2026. Facts were read from official Delaware pages via the web index; where that standard was not met, the item is flagged VERIFY, UNAVAILABLE or SECONDARY ONLY. Federal baseline is applied as given in the research brief and is not re-researched here. Run 2 found no primary official source contradicting any Run 1 baseline fact; the only official-text tension is internal to OMC's own pages (MCA effective date — see Combined Notes B1).

15%
Retail marijuana tax (4 Del.C. §1382) — Delaware has no general state sales tax; medical sales exempt
1 Aug 2025
First legal adult-use sales, via converted compassion centres
125
Statutory lottery licences: 60 cultivation / 30 manufacturing / 30 retail / 5 testing, plus conversions
$7,354,293
Total retail sales, first month (Aug 2025) — flower 55%
No home grow
Home cultivation prohibited for adult-use and medical; public consumption prohibited
0.3%
Hemp THC cap — post-decarboxylation (total THC) basis, 3 DE Admin. Code 805
Federal baseline applied (given, not researched). Split scheduling under final rule 91 FR 22714, effective 28 April 2026: FDA-approved products and state-licensed medical marijuana are in Schedule III; all other marijuana, including all adult-use, remains Schedule I; the broader 2024 rescheduling proposal is not final. IRC §280E generally falls away only for businesses no longer dealing in Schedule I/II substances; adult-use activity remains fully subject to 280E. Hemp: PL 119-37 §781 rewrites the federal hemp definition to a total-THC standard (incl. THCA) with a 0.4 mg total THC per-container cap for final hemp-derived cannabinoid products — legal effect 12 Nov 2026, but PL 119-103 §2019 limits it until 11 Dec 2026 to only the provisions excluding cannabinoids that are not naturally produced or are synthesised; the full definition applies after 11 Dec 2026. USDA has delayed enforcement of the DEA-registered-laboratory hemp testing requirement until 31 Dec 2026. The federal medical-marijuana appropriations rider is in force through 11 Dec 2026. No federal cannabis banking law has been enacted. Where a Delaware source differs in wording, it is flagged, not reconciled.
AUDIT

Independent Audit — 24 October 2026

Last audited: 24 October 2026. This audit was done by a separate fact-checking agent who did not write the original report. Primary sources only were used for confirmation: the Delaware Code, Delaware Administrative Code / regulations.delaware.gov, General Assembly bill pages, the Office of the Marijuana Commissioner, the Division of Revenue, the Department of Agriculture, Delaware open-data portals, and official county and municipal codes. News, law-firm blogs, trade press and AI summaries were never used to confirm a fact; where only such a source exists the verdict is Unverified. The federal baseline in the commission was used as given and was not re-audited: 91 FR 22714 (effective 28 April 2026) puts FDA-approved products and state-licensed medical marijuana in Schedule III and leaves all other marijuana, including all adult-use, in Schedule I; 280E falls away generally only for businesses no longer dealing in Schedule I/II substances; hemp PL 119-37 §781 takes legal effect 12 November 2026 but PL 119-103 §2019 limits it until 11 December 2026 to only the provisions excluding cannabinoids that are not naturally produced or are synthesised, with the full total-THC definition and 0.4 mg per container cap applying after 11 December 2026; USDA enforcement of the DEA-registered-laboratory hemp testing requirement is delayed until 31 December 2026; the federal medical-marijuana appropriations rider is in force through 11 December 2026; no federal cannabis banking law has been enacted. Any statement contradicting that baseline has been corrected to match it.

How to read the verdicts. Confirmed = verified against a primary source named in the row. Corrected = wrong, incomplete or misattributed, and the corrected wording is what the rewritten report now says. Removed = wrong with no correct version found, or a repealed provision presented as current law. Unverified = no primary source was opened in this audit; the claim is kept only with a flag, or not relied on. Confirmed claims are grouped where the original text was entirely correct; every material correction, removal and unresolved flag is listed individually. The rewritten report below applies every correction in place. No outdated figure is left standing in the main text; where a figure could not be resolved, the report says so and does not pick a winner. No personal phone number or email for an individual licence holder was found in the report to redact; business/agency contacts only are retained, and no contact detail has been imported from a secondary site (notably, no phone number for any testing lab has been added).

Part 0 / Part 215% retail tax, medical exempt; 7% to Justice Reinvestment FundConfirmed (precision corrected)4 Del.C. §§1382, 138615% and the medical exemption confirmed (§1382). The 7% rule is: “Each month, 7% of total marijuana tax money received from the preceding month shall be withdrawn from the Marijuana Regulation Fund and credited to the Justice Reinvestment Fund” (§1386(a)(2)).
Part 0 / Part 41,269 paid applications; 1,271 received / 1,256 to lottery; 125 licences “awarded in December”; “99 licences in October”CorrectedOMC 3 Oct 2024; OMC 13 Dec 2024These are stages, not rivals: 1,269 paid (3 Oct 2024; fees $4,069,000), 1,271 received, 1,256 proceeded to the lottery phase (Feb 2025 release), 727 advanced to the 24 Oct lottery (Open Retail excluded). 125 is the total across both lotteries (24 Oct + 19 Dec 2024); the December lottery alone was the Open Retail lottery: 529 eligible applicants for 15 licences (New Castle 7, Kent 3, Sussex 5). “99 licences in October” is secondary-only and is deleted. The 2-application received-vs-paid gap is unexplained by OMC and stays flagged. The 1,269 figure is from OMC’s 3 Oct 2024 release, not a July 2025 release (July 2025 restates it).

Part 0 / Part 1.5HB 373 signed 23 July 2026; endorsement effective 21 Oct 2026; beverage tax from 1 Feb 2027: $0.50/single-serving, $8.50/750 mLConfirmed, with sunset addedomc.delaware.gov/infused-beverages; OABCC Fall 2026 communicationDates and rates confirmed (a secondary “22 July” signing date is wrong). Added: HB 373 contains a federal-trigger termination — if the federal government closes the “hemp loophole” in December 2026 so that an HB 373 beverage would be classified as a controlled substance under federal law, the law expires (OABCC, official). The trigger is the federal classification, not automatically the 12 Nov / 11 Dec 2026 dates. Beverage tax is indicated as Title 4 §581A (per HB 373 amendment text), not Title 30 — §581A text itself remains VERIFY.
Part 0 / Part 1.9 / Part 12.9SB 226 signed 21 May 2026; hospitals must permit terminally ill patients to use medical marijuana; effective date “21 May 2027 VERIFY”Confirmed (flag removed)legis.delaware.gov SB 226; 85 Del. Laws c. 268 (Ryan’s Law)Flag removed: Signed 21 May 2026, 85 Del. Laws c. 268, effective 21 May 2027 (1 year after enactment). It is not current law at the audit date. From 21 May 2027: designated-caregiver administration of medical marijuana oil only to a minor patient on a school bus / school grounds (school nurse/employee only if also parent/legal guardian, day’s doses on their person, in Delaware only); a licensed general hospital must permit non-smoked/non-vaped use by a terminally ill cardholder, subject to medical-contraindication and federal-enforcement suspension rights. Until then the pre-SB 226 ban in §4904A(a)(2) remains law. Smoking/vaping on school grounds is prohibited in all versions.

SectionClaimVerdictPrimary source (URL)Corrected wording
Part 1.5 / Part 7Hemp: statute ≤0.3% delta-9; Reg. 805 post-decarboxylation = total THC via 0.877 formula; sampling, $350/sample; USDA plan 27 Jan 2020; fees; “whichever is greater / 7 U.S.C. §5940” definition; remediation for over-limit lotsCorrected3 DE Admin. Code 805; State News 28 Jan 2020Statute §2801(2) confirmed. Sampling confirmed: one pre-harvest sample per lot (§6.1.1), $350 per sample (§6.1.2), second sample if harvest not completed within 15 days (§6.1.3), producer notice 30–15 days before anticipated harvest (§6.1.5). Corrections: (1) The 0.877 formula is §1302(40) for marijuana; nothing in 805 §§5–6 as retrieved states it for hemp — write “post-decarboxylation basis under 805 §6.0” (definition in 805 §3 — VERIFY), not “delta-9 + THCA×0.877” as the hemp rule. (2) The “whichever is greater / §5940” definition does not match §2801 and must not be presented as the statutory definition. (3) There is no remediation provision in Reg. 805 — over-limit lots must be destroyed on-site within 72 hours of notification (§805-8.1) or disposed of where on-site destruction is not feasible (§805-8.2), non-retrievable and unfit for commerce; a producer does not commit a negligent violation if it made reasonable efforts and post-decarboxylated delta-9 is ≤0.5%, but destruction/disposal is still required (§805-8.4.1.1). “Remediation” is a 5001 marijuana concept and is removed for hemp. USDA approval: announced 28 Jan 2020, “second state on the East Coast”, Delaware assumes primary regulatory responsibility, FDA retains authority over hemp products for human/animal consumption; the 27 Jan date itself is secondary-reported (announced 28 Jan) and is noted as such. The ams-prod.azureedge.us PDF URL was not verified — replace with the USDA state-plan list / DDA hemp page.

Delaware has responded legislatively: HB 373 (enacted, hemp delta-9 beverages, federal sunset above) and HB 395 (House-passed 17 June 2026, Senate pending — see Part 10 row). What remains true: no executive (Governor/OMC/DDA) statement applying PL 119-37/PL 119-103 to Delaware was found, and Delaware has not conformed its 0.3% delta-9 production standard to a federal total-THC product standard on sources read.

Part 2.015% levy in “30 Del.C. Ch. 29A / §2901 et seq.”; “VERIFY / PREMISE NOT CONFIRMED”Removed (premise disproved)4 Del.C. §§1381–1389The levy, collection, funds and distribution are in 4 Del.C. §§1381–1389 (Subch. VIII). Title 30 matters only collaterally: Ch. 5 assessment/collection/appeal (§1383(d)), §368 confidentiality (§1383(f)), business licences Title 30 Ch. 21, wholesaler/retailer GRT provisions (§§2701, 2901) determining the Revenue licence category. No “Ch. 29A” marijuana tax exists. The VERIFY flag on this premise is removed — the premise is disproved by the DelCode structure itself.
Part 2.1§1382: tax on retail sale, not on medical (§1382(a)); 15% (§1382(b)); rounded UP to next cent (§1382(c)); separately stated (§1382(d)); no discount/free-bundle (§1382(f) “pre-Oct 2026”); Oct version excludes infused beverages from 15%Corrected (§1382(f) qualifier)4 Del.C. §1382 (both versions)(a), (b), (c), (d) and the Oct (a) beverage exclusion confirmed verbatim. Delete “(§1382(f) pre-Oct 2026)” — the ban was NOT removed in Oct 2026.
Part 2.1Collection: consumer tax, retailer as taxpayer/trustee (§1383(a)); monthly report to Commissioner, Revenue and DATE (§1383(b)); portal return due 15th; penalties 1%/month to 25% (failure to pay), 5%/month to 50% (failure to file), interest 0.5%/month; records 3 years (§1384); civil penalty ≤$1,000Confirmed4 Del.C. §§1383–1384; Revenue FAQAll confirmed. (2) §1383(a) says the tax “is considered a tax upon the retail licensee that is required to collect the tax, and the licensee is considered a taxpayer” while ALSO being “imposed on the consumer” — quote both halves; do not imply the consumer files. Cash payment refused (FAQ, citing §5001-14.4).
Part 2.1Delaware has no general sales tax; $100 product = $115 at register; medical bears no point-of-sale tax (presented as “general background”)Confirmed (upgraded)Division of Revenue (no state/local sales tax); 4 Del.C. §1382Upgraded from background note to primary-confirmed (Division of Revenue). $100 → $115 arithmetic confirmed for an adult-use marijuana product alone. Nuance added: gross receipts tax is a seller-side tax (not added at the register), and marijuana products are exempt from retail GRT (see §2.3) — so $115 is not understated by a hidden seller tax on that product. Non-marijuana items at a retailer are not subject to the retail marijuana tax (FAQ) but are outside this $115 example.

Part 3.1–3.23 counties; municipalities govern inside boundaries and may prohibit outright; counties govern unincorporated areas only; opt-out not opt-in; §1351 quoted; §1351A quoted; no state moratorium; no local licence categoryConfirmed4 Del.C. §§1351, 1351A, 1354; 4 Del.C. §1383(e)Keep, with two clarifications: (1) The §1351A hours floor (Mon–Sat 9 a.m.–9 p.m., Sun noon–8 p.m.), the ½-mile/500-ft ceilings, conversion nonconforming use, building-permit bar, and indoor fully-enclosed cultivation in agricultural/industrial zones bind counties only — they do not override a municipality’s §1351 powers (e.g. Georgetown’s Sunday ban survives). (2) §1351A(a)(3) says the section “preempts and supersedes all existing and future county ordinances” — quote that broad text, not a softened “in part” without explanation; the workable harmonising reading is that regimes exceeding §1351A parameters are superseded while consistent county limits survive under (a)(1). §1351A(d) county retail prohibition in commercial/industrial zones is limited to within ½ mile of another retail store or 500 ft of a school/higher-education institution, licensed childcare facility (14 Del.C. ch. 30A), residential treatment facility (§2203 [repealed], Title 16), park or library. Part 3.5New Castle County: retail in commercial areas, 1,000 ft from sensitive areas, 1 mile between dispensaries (secondary summaries); “1-mile matches §1354(e) rural rule; 1,000-ft exceeds §1351A(d) 500-ft ceiling — VERIFY”Confirmed in substance (1,000 ft upgraded); Corrected (§1354(e) figures)New Castle County Board of Adjustment project record (UDC §40.03.341); 4 Del.C. §1354(e)Corrected: “New Castle County UDC §40.03.341 requires 1,000 feet from sensitive parcels (including churches, substance-abuse treatment facilities and government offices) for retail marijuana stores, as shown in an official county project record; the 1-mile retail-to-retail spacing is reported for the county code. The full UDC text was not opened, so the complete sensitive-use list and any commercial-zone limitation remain to be confirmed against the UDC itself.” State spacing under §1354(e) is 1,200 feet in incorporated cities/towns and 1 mile in unincorporated/rural areas, measured by accessible public road or street — so a county 1-mile rule matches the state rule only for unincorporated areas, not for incorporated areas (where the state figure is 1,200 feet). A county 1,000-ft sensitive-use buffer exceeds §1351A(d)’s 500-ft ceiling for county prohibitions and is therefore superseded to that extent as a matter of state law; the same 1,000-ft figure in a municipal ordinance (e.g. Georgetown) is not governed by §1351A(d) at all. §1354(e) exemptions (existing licences, transfers/renewals, moves within 500 ft, shopping-centre/mall moves) and the >9/10-mile discretionary grant are retained.

Ownership cap confirmed (§1344(a): no person, incl. a person with a financial interest, may own/operate more than 1 establishment of the same licence type in a single county; §1344(b): different types in one county permitted). County assignment: flag removed — OMC 22 Sept 2025: “Contrary to recent reports, county-based assignments have not been removed. Licensees [continue to be assigned to specific counties]… limited flexibility to consider reassignments” on documentation of efforts in the original county, evidence of site control in the new location, verification of compliance with OMC location-based regulations, and zoning confirmation from the local government of the proposed site. Note: county assignment is OMC lottery/policy design (shown also by the Dec 2024 Open Retail county splits, 7/3/5) — §1343 itself states no per-county quotas, and §1344 states a per-county ownership cap, not an allocation quota. Do not present county quotas as statutory. §1343 pools schedule (cultivation from 1 Nov 2024, manufacturing from 1 Dec 2024, retail/testing from 1 Mar 2025) and §1343(g) excess issuance after 1 Aug 2025 if needed/under-filled confirmed. Applications accepted 19 Aug–30 Sept 2024 (statute said Commissioner “may begin” 1 Sept 2024, §1343(a) — OMC opened early; no OMC explanation for the early opening was located).
Part 4.1, Lotteries / conditional / active / SE programme24 Oct + 19 Dec 2024 lotteries; 529 for 15 retail; selected-applicant background docs due 10 Feb 2025; conditional 18 months (§1302(4); §1345); active requirements §1346; SE eligibility §1336, technical assistance §1338, financial assistance §1339; SEFA grants; no loan programme; DIA map/form; startup costs — UNAVAILABLEConfirmed, except 10 Feb 2025 and DIA URLs — Unverified; §1302(4) citation correctedOMC 23 Dec 2024; 4 Del.C. §§1336, 1338–1339, 1345–134619 Dec 2024 lottery sharpened: it was the Open Retail lottery only (529 eligible for 15). 24 Oct 2024 covered all other categories (727 applications advanced, Open Retail excluded; four categories required no lottery because applications matched or fell below available licences: Open Testing Lab, Social Equity Testing Lab, Micro Manufacturing–Sussex, Micro Cultivation–New Castle — OMC 3 Oct 2024). Delete any sentence implying 125 licences were awarded in December alone. 10 Feb 2025 background-documentation deadline: Unverified — not stated in the OMC releases opened; do not present it as an OMC-stated deadline unless the Feb 2025 release text is re-opened and the date found there. Conditional licence: cite §1345 alone for the 18-month rule (identify a physical location, become operational within 18 months, premises approved by the Commissioner, extension only on good-faith efforts, otherwise Commissioner “must rescind”); the §1302(4) citation does not support it in the text read (§1342, the former conditional-licence section, was repealed — see §1339 row). Active requirements §1346(a)(1)–(5) confirmed (background-check fees paid, financial/ownership docs approved, labour peace attestation, local zoning/fire/building compliance, any additional Commissioner requirements). SE eligibility confirmed (§1336(1) ≥51% ownership/control by individuals who resided ≥5 of preceding 15 years in a DIA, or were convicted/adjudicated delinquent of a Delaware marijuana offence before 23 Apr 2023 (except delivery to a minor or Tier 3 quantity under 16 Del.C. §4751C), or have a parent/legal guardian/child/spouse/dependent so convicted — keep both qualifiers “under Delaware law” and “prior to April 23, 2023”). §1338 technical assistance (may partner with Division of Small Business) confirmed. §1339 confirmed with a stale cross-reference flagged: §1339(a) investigation duty incl. “portions of the Justice Reinvestment Fund under §1387”; §1339(b) as printed still cites §1342, but §1342 was repealed by 84 Del. Laws c. 301 §12, effective 17 July 2024 — read §1339(b) as referring to conditional licensees under §1345. SEFA: Sept 2025 position is stronger than “will soon begin” — OMC announced implementation on 11 Sept 2025 with distribution commencing in the coming weeks as milestones are reached; eligibility limited to approved social equity licensees; amounts vary by licence type; milestone-based phased disbursement; restricted to approved business expenses. No loan programme (negative finding, §§1338–1339 contain no loan programme). DIA map and Social Equity Eligibility Validation Form URLs: Unverified (neither retrieved from an official OMC page). Startup costs: no official estimate — UNAVAILABLE; official cost floor = application fee + biennial licence fee + conversion fee (if applicable) + background-check/badge/local permit costs, excl. premises, build-out, security, insurance, staffing (§1347(e): person required to obtain a background check “is responsible for any costs associated with obtaining the background check”).

Revenue goes to the Marijuana Regulation Fund, with 7% of each month’s tax receipts transferred monthly to the Justice Reinvestment Fund; after administrative costs and a $5m maximum unencumbered Regulation Fund balance, the remainder goes to the General Fund. There is no cannabis-specific county/municipal tax under current law (§1383(e)).” Also add: “From 21 Oct 2026, §1382 does not impose the 15% tax on retail sales of infused beverages by a package store or marijuana retail store (separate beverage tax: $0.50/single-serving container, $8.50/750 mL bottle, from 1 Feb 2027 — OMC beverage page).” No gross-receipts-tax impact specific to cannabis was found in §§1382–1387 as read — do not invent one.

Part 10.2Interstate commerce: OMC FAQ verbatim — “No. Marijuana remains illegal under federal law, and you may not carry or transport marijuana over state lines. It remains illegal to leave (or enter) Delaware with any marijuana products – medical or adult-use… Mailing marijuana in or out of the State is also illegal.”; “No Delaware interstate cannabis commerce law, compact, or agreement was found. Nothing in 4 Del.C. Ch. 13 or Reg. 5001 as read authorises import/export, an interstate compact, or adult-use reciprocity”; Medical reciprocity is the sole cross-border consumer mechanism, one-way in-state purchase, not interstate commerce: out-of-state patients may purchase via visiting-patient application (QR code at dispensary; upload home-state licence + medical card; Delaware out-of-state ID card expiring with home card), while “Only Delaware residents can apply for the Delaware Medical Marijuana Program” (OMC FAQ). Visiting patients may not take Delaware product home across a state line; “Hemp may move interstate under the 2018 Farm Bill framework, but Delaware creates no interstate hemp-commerce agreement; in-state transport/storage is via the handler licence (Part 7) — no Delaware hemp compact found [UNAVAILABLE]”Confirmed (FAQ position, no compact, reciprocity); Corrected (FAQ rationale sentence, handler premise)omc.delaware.gov/faq; 3 DE Admin. Part 12.2Driving: “no numeric per se THC limit” / “impairment-based”; medical metabolites shield §4904A(a)(4); vehicle consumption; closed-container rule; DelDOT warning; no THC breathalyser; implied consentCorrected (prong structure confirmed; “impairment-based” alone misleading; metabolites shield dated; vehicle-consumption authority misattributed)21 Del.C. §4177 (DelCode Title 21 is the primary target; wording confirmed via republication — final report must cite DelCode, not Justia); SB 226, 85 Del. Laws c. 268Driving corrected: “There is no numeric THC blood limit in Delaware law — that headline is correct. But Delaware is not purely impairment-based: §4177 has two relevant prongs — (a)(2) impairment (‘under the influence of any drug’), and (a)(6) an any-amount-within-4-hours prong that applies only where the drug use was unlawful (lawful adult-use/medical use, proved by the defendant under (c)(8), is outside (a)(6); inactive metabolites alone are excluded by (c)(9)). Delete the ‘statute not opened / VERIFY prong structure’ flag and replace it with this two-prong statement. DUI penalty figures may now be cited from §4177(d) (first offence $500–$1,500 and/or ≤12 months) — the report’s decision to give no penalty figures remains safe, but the figures are no longer secondary-only.” Also: §1305 preserves DUI law — “Nothing in this chapter is intended to allow driving under the influence of marijuana…” — and the HB 373 version effective 21 Oct 2026 adds marijuana products/infused beverages. (a)(6) detail: “blood contains, within 4 hours of driving, any amount of an illicit or recreational drug that is the result of the unlawful use or consumption…”; (b)(1) legal entitlement is not a defence (except (b)(3) prescription defence); (c)(11) “while under the influence” = less able, mentally or physically, to exercise clear judgment, sufficient physical control, or due care; (c)(8) burden on defendant to show lawful use under (a)(6); (c)(9) inactive metabolites/ingredients excluded from (a)(6). Metabolites shield corrected: “Until 21 May 2027, the shield is in its pre-SB 226 form (‘…metabolites or components … that appear in insufficient concentration to cause impairment’). From 21 May 2027 (SB 226 effective date), the qualifier is removed: a registered qualifying patient shall not be considered under the influence solely because of the presence of metabolites or components of marijuana. Do not present the SB 226 version as current law before 21 May 2027.” Vehicle consumption corrected: “Do not cite §4177J for marijuana vehicle consumption — as enacted it is alcohol-only (a 2026 proposal to add marijuana to §4177J was located in legis documents but was not confirmed as enacted). Vehicle/public consumption is prohibited via §4904A(a)(3) (medical), §4764(d) (public/moving vehicle), and §6.5.3.2/§1334(e) (on-premises bans). Part 12.3Expungement layers (SB 197 / SB 37 / Clean Slate): mandatory/automatic expungement for low-level marijuana possession in several layers (all summaries secondary, consistent — session-law verification VERIFY): 2018 SB 197 — mandatory eligibility for single conviction for ≤1 oz; 2019 SB 37 — single cannabis misdemeanour expungeable after 5 years, single felony after 7 years; 2021 Clean Slate laws — all misdemeanour cannabis-possession convictions eligible for mandatory expungement, drug felonies after 10 years, with process automatic from mid-2024 (1 Aug 2024 per ODS summary) — though incomplete digitisation means records can be missed, so petition remains safer route; §4764(i) under-21 expungement: person convicted of a single §4764(c) offence while under 21 may, on reaching 21, apply for expungement of conviction + arrest indicia; order “shall issue” on proof of age 21 unless person failed to comply with sentencing order or has another outstanding §4764 charge ($100 SBI administrative fee + civil filing fee); “HB 150 §4770” claim — VERIFY / POSSIBLE MIS-CITATION; Justice Reinvestment Fund 7% (4 Del.C. §§1381, 1386)Corrected in large part (SB 197, Clean Slate now primary-confirmed); Removed (§4764(i) as stated); Unverified (SB 37 detail, digitisation claim); Corrected (Justice Fund pinpoint)SB 197, 149th GA; SB 111, Clean Slate Act; 4 Del.C. §1386(a)(2)Expungement replaced: “SB 197 (2018) added §4764(j): a person convicted before 18 December 2015 of a single pre-decriminalisation marijuana possession/use/consumption offence is eligible for mandatory expungement under §4373, if otherwise eligible and if it is the applicant’s only conviction (approved 29 Aug 2018; synopsis: conviction must be applicant’s only criminal conviction). PART 1

Laws

1.1 Legal status

  • Medical: Legal since 2011 under the Delaware Medical Marijuana Act, 16 Del.C. Ch. 49A (§§4901A–4928A). Source: chapter range listing, LawServer index of Delaware Code Title 16 — lawserver.com (secondary index of official code) VERIFY DIRECT DELCODE CH. 49A PAGE
  • Adult-use possession: Legal for 21+ from HB 1 (84 Del. Laws, c. 15), codified principally at 16 Del.C. §§4701, 4764, 4764A. OMC: “Delaware’s adult-use marijuana law permits adults aged 21 and over to possess up to one ounce of marijuana, or 750 milligrams of concentrated THC products… public consumption and home cultivation remain prohibited.” Source: OMC Adult Use — omc.delaware.gov/adult-use OFFICIAL
  • Adult-use commerce: Delaware Marijuana Control Act, 4 Del.C. Ch. 13 (HB 2, 84 Del. Laws, c. 24). OMC states the Act “became effective on July 5, 2023” (the effective date OMC gives; HB 1/HB 2 were allowed to become law without Governor Carney’s signature in April 2023 — SECONDARY SOURCES ONLY — VERIFY on legis.delaware.gov bill pages). Source: OMC — omc.delaware.gov OFFICIAL
  • Decriminalisation history: Pre-2023, possession of a personal-use quantity was a $100 civil penalty (2015, HB 39); the current scheme in §4764 retains civil penalties for under-21 possession (see §1.3). Do not present the 2015 civil-penalty regime as the current adult rule — for 21+ within the personal-use quantity there is no civil or criminal penalty under §4764A(b).

1.2 Statutes and regulations

Delaware Code Online shows, for Ch. 13, dual versions of many sections: “[Effective until Oct. 21, 2026]” and “[Effective Oct. 21, 2026]” (the HB 373 / 85 Del. Laws, c. 394 version). Both are distinguished where material. “Last amended” = latest Del. Laws citation printed at the foot of the section on DelCode as read 24 Oct 2026. Official links — Subch. I sc01 · Subch. III sc03 · Subch. IV sc04 · Subch. VIII sc08 — all OFFICIAL. Subchapters II (§§1321–1327, Commissioner/Appeals Commission), V (§§1361–1364, cancellation/suspension), VI (§§1366–1367, transfer/death) and VII (§§1371–1375, ownership/financial interests) were not opened in full in this run — contents VERIFY beyond the Appeals Commission procedure in §1352 and enforcement in §1310 quoted below.

A. 4 Del.C. Ch. 13 — The Delaware Marijuana Control Act (§§1301–1389)

SectionSummary & operative textLast amended
§1301 Purpose and findingsPersonal use legal for 21+, taxed like alcohol, regulated like alcohol.

“the personal use of marijuana should be legal for persons 21 years of age or older and taxed in a manner similar to alcohol.” — §1301(a)

84 Del. Laws, c. 24, §4
§1302 DefinitionsDefines conditional licence (18 months), conversion licence, DIA, infused beverage / delta-9 extract / infused beverage endorsement (Oct 2026 version), microbusiness, open, social equity, “Total THC.”

“‘Total THC’ means the sum of the percentage by weight of tetrahydrocannabinolic acid, multiplied by 0.877, plus the percentage of weight of THC.” — §1302(40) [Effective Oct. 21, 2026]

“‘Infused beverage’ means a beverage… [that] contains… total delta-9 extract that does not exceed 10 milligrams per infused beverage container… [and] does not exceed 170 milligrams in a 750 milliliter bottle.” — §1302(15) [Effective Oct. 21, 2026]

85 Del. Laws, c. 518, §12; c. 394, §1 (Oct 2026 version)

“Nothing in this chapter is intended to impact or impose any requirement or restriction on employers with respect to terms and conditions of employment including accommodation, policies, or discipline.”

See §1.8 for the contrasting medical protection.		84 Del. Laws, c. 514, §2
§1305–1308§1305 DUI: nothing allows driving under the influence / while impaired. §1306 Under-21: transfer to under-21 not permitted; knowingly entering/remaining in a licensed establishment by under-21 is a civil offence, $50 penalty. §1307 Private property: owners/occupiers may prohibit/regulate marijuana on their property; residential landlords may prohibit possession or non-smoked consumption only in the three listed cases (landlord-resident ≤3 rooms/tenants; detention/medical/educational etc. residence; federal-law/benefit loss). §1308 Unlawful extraction: extraction by non-manufacturers using solvents other than water, glycerin, propylene glycol, vegetable oil or food-grade ethanol, or ethanol extraction near open flame, is a class G felony.84 Del. Laws, c. 24 (as read)
§1309 Lawful operation; hoursLicensed activity is lawful and not a basis for seizure/forfeiture. Retail hours (both versions):

“A holder of license for a retail marijuana store may not sell or deliver marijuana or marijuana products on Thanksgiving, Easter, or Christmas or between the hours of 10:00 p.m. and 9:00 a.m. on Mondays through Saturdays, and on Sundays before noon or after 8:00 p.m.” — §1309(b)(2)

Sunday sales require a special licence, $500 biennially (§1309(b)(3)); Oct–Dec early opening: 8 a.m. Fri–Sat, 10 a.m. Sun. Municipalities ≥50,000 population may limit Sunday sales to max 4 hours and set earlier weekday closing by ordinance. Marijuana may not be sold in an establishment licensed to sell alcoholic liquors (§1309(c)) — except infused beverages under §1309A from 21 Oct 2026, sold by package stores in a separate designated section.		84 Del. B. 16 Del.C. Ch. 49A — Delaware Medical Marijuana Act (§§4901A–4928A)

Registered qualifying patients protected if possessing ≤6 oz usable marijuana (§4903A(a)); designated caregivers ≤6 oz per connected patient (§4903A(b)); visiting qualifying patients protected ≤6 oz (§4903A(c)); discrimination protections §4905A (quoted in §1.8); compassion centres registered under §4914A (cross-ref. 4 Del.C. §1302(3)); renewal eligibility §4916A (cross-ref. conversion §1335A(a)(1)). The full current DelCode chapter page was not opened — VERIFY for sections not quoted. HB 285 (2024) amendments — provider may certify any diagnosed condition likely to benefit; 65+ self-certification; 1/2/3-year cards, indefinite for terminal illness; out-of-state reciprocity/purchasing; CBD-rich and compassionate-use programmes eliminated as unnecessary — are stated in the Senate Democrats official release and secondary sources; the amended statutory text itself was not opened in this run, so treat HB 285 mechanics as official-legislative-summary, statute text VERIFY. Source: senatedems.delaware.gov (official legislative caucus). Last-amended dates per section UNAVAILABLE IN THIS RUN.

C. 16 Del.C. Ch. 47 — Uniform Controlled Substances Act (relevant sections)

  • §4701 “Personal use quantity”:

    “1 ounce or less of marijuana in the form of leaf marijuana, 12 grams or less of concentrated cannabis, or cannabis products containing 750 milligrams or less of delta-9-tetrahydrocannabinol.”

    (via LawServer extract of §4701; DelCode direct page not opened VERIFY)
  • §4764 Possession of marijuana. Over-personal-use-quantity use/consumption/possession = unclassified misdemeanour, fine ≤$575, imprisonment ≤3 months (§4764(b) extract). Under-21 personal-use-quantity: civil penalty $100 first, $200–$500 second, unclassified misdemeanour + $100 fine third+ (§4764(c) extract). Public/moving-vehicle consumption is punished under §4764(d) (current penalty: unclassified misdemeanour, ≤$200 / ≤5 days — SECONDARY SOURCES ONLY — VERIFY; HB 252/HS 1 in 2026 would downgrade it to a civil violation $50/$100 — see §1.9, not enacted as of the sources read).
  • §4764A Legal marijuana-related activity (84 Del. Laws, c. 15, §1):

    “no civil or criminal penalty may be imposed, nor may criminal or civil asset forfeiture be pursued, for any of the following activities by an individual 21 years of age or older: (1) Adult sharing of a personal use quantity or less of marijuana. (2) Possessing, using, displaying, purchasing, or transporting marijuana accessories or a personal use quantity or less of marijuana outside of a motor vehicle. (3) Possessing and transporting… inside of a motor vehicle as long as the marijuana… is in a closed container or is not readily accessible to anyone inside the motor vehicle. (4) Assisting another individual who is 21 years of age or older in any of the acts described…” — §4764A(b)

    “The following acts remain unlawful…: (1) Consuming marijuana in an area accessible to the public or in a moving vehicle… (2) Growing, manufacturing, selling, or cultivating marijuana without a license granted under Chapter 49A of this title or other provision of Delaware law…” — §4764A(c)

    Source for §§4701/4764/4764A extracts: LawServer — lawserver.com (secondary reproduction of official code) VERIFY ON DELCODE

D. Hemp statutes — 3 Del.C. Ch. 28; 3 Del.C. §101(11)

Ch. 28 Industrial Hemp (§§2800–2803), 79 Del. Laws, c. 369, §1 (no later amendment shown in the text read):

“This act shall be known and may be cited as the ‘Industrial Hemp Research Act.’” — §2800

“‘Industrial hemp’ means the plant Cannabis sativa L. and any part of such plant, whether growing or not, with a delta-9 tetrahydrocannabinol concentration of not more than 0.3 percent on a dry weight basis.” — §2801(2)

§2802: Department of Agriculture may grow/cultivate for agricultural/academic research and certify higher-education institutions (location, research plan, supervising employee); Department maintains the certified-institution list. §2803 VERIFY TEXT — NOT READ. 3 Del.C. §101(11) (via SB 266 session-law text, legis.delaware.gov): Department may “[a]dopt any policies and regulations necessary to permit the cultivation of industrial hemp when federal law permits the cultivation of industrial hemp beyond agricultural or academic research.” This is the authority under which the commercial Domestic Hemp Production Program operates despite Ch. 28’s research-only wording — flag the apparent tension; do not read Ch. 28 alone as limiting Delaware hemp to research in 2026. Official link for Ch. 28 text as read: Justia reproduction + DelCode PDF proxy VERIFY DIRECT DELCODE TITLE 3 CH. 28 PAGE — NOT RETURNED BY SEARCH.

1.3 Possession limits, home grow, public consumption, product types

ItemRuleSource

Home growProhibited for adult-use AND for medical patients/caregivers. Unlicensed growing/manufacturing/selling/cultivating remains unlawful (§4764A(c)(2)); OMC states home cultivation “remain[s] prohibited.” No home-grow authorisation exists in Ch. 13 or Ch. 49A as read.OMC Adult Use; §4764A(c)

Retail may sell only: retail marijuana, retail marijuana products, accessories, nonconsumable products (apparel), marijuana-related products (childproof containers); no cigarettes/alcohol, no non-marijuana edibles (sodas, candies, baked goods), no nicotine/alcohol-containing marijuana products (§1332(j)).DelCode §1332; State News

1.4 Medical programme

  • Administration: Now under OMC. 4 Del.C. §1321 duty: “Oversee and administer the Delaware Medical Marijuana Act under Chapter 49A of Title 16.” The premise that DHSS/Division of Public Health currently runs the programme is outdated — DHSS links in 2025 State News releases point to the medical programme, but regulatory authority has transferred to OMC VERIFY CURRENT DHSS vs OMC DIVISION OF CARD-ISSUANCE OPERATIONS — OMC site has a Medical Marijuana section; DHSS page not opened in this run.
  • Qualifying conditions (current law after HB 285, 2024): No closed list controls. A healthcare provider (MD, DO, APRN, PA licensed in Delaware — provider types: SECONDARY ONLY — VERIFY ON STATUTE) may certify a patient with any diagnosed medical condition the provider determines the patient is likely to receive therapeutic/palliative benefit from marijuana. The pre-HB 285 “debilitating medical condition” list (cancer, HIV/AIDS, decompensated cirrhosis, ALS, Alzheimer’s agitation, PTSD, autism with aggressive behaviour, glaucoma, chronic debilitating migraine, terminal illness, plus symptom categories: cachexia/wasting, severe debilitating pain unresponsive >3 months or with serious side-effects from other treatment, intractable nausea, seizures, severe/persistent muscle spasms incl. MS) remains in the statute as the definition floor — do not present it as an exhaustive gate. Sources: §4902A extracts via search; HB 285 summary, Senate Democrats (official, above).
  • 65+ self-certification: Patients 65+ may self-certify without provider written certification (HB 285). Card terms/fees: 1-year $50, 2-year $75, 3-year $100; terminal illness = indefinite/permanent card; low-income $25 fee reported FEES: SECONDARY ONLY — VERIFY ON OMC MEDICAL PAGE; NOT OPENED IN THIS RUN. Patient counts reported July 2024: 48,406 active patients, 1,427 caregivers, 2,576 certifying practitioners — SECONDARY (Hemp Gazette reporting programme figures) — Verify against OMC annual report.
  • Minors: Under-18 cards only in narrow cases (terminal-illness pain/anxiety/depression; intractable epilepsy/seizure disorder; chronic/debilitating disease with failed treatment for cachexia, intractable nausea, or severe painful persistent spasms; Dept.-added conditions; compassionate-use eligibility) and minors “may only receive marijuana oil” (§4909A(b)–(c) as amended, via legis.delaware.gov session-law extract — legis.delaware.gov OFFICIAL). Paediatric certifier specialty rules VERIFY.
  • Caregivers: Registered designated caregivers protected ≤6 oz per connected patient (§4903A(b)); may receive compensation for costs. Caregiver age/registration mechanics VERIFY FULL TEXT.
  • Reciprocity: Visiting qualifying patients (non-resident or resident <30 days, with valid out-of-state/territory/country-recognised card) protected ≤6 oz (§4902A(18)/§4903A(c) extracts). HB 285 additionally authorises out-of-state cardholders to purchase in Delaware dispensaries (Senate Democrats summary). Pre-HB 285 law protected possession/use but purchasing mechanics were unclear — flag the distinction.
  • Compassion centres ↔ adult-use conversion: See Part 4 (§§1335A–1335D). Conversion retailers must continue operating as a medical dispensing location, prioritise patients (suspend recreational sales in a shortage), maintain/increase medical product diversity, and file max-price lists (§1335B). Conversion retail attestation: tax-free sales to patients/caregivers, 3 oz/14 days & 6 oz/month, home delivery to qualifying patients (§1335A(a)(7)).
  • Federal Schedule III — Delaware official position: Only the OMC 18 Dec 2025 conditional statement (Part 0) was found. No Delaware official statement applying the given split baseline (medical = Schedule III / adult-use = Schedule I, 91 FR 22714) to Delaware licensees was found — UNAVAILABLE. Do not attribute the baseline’s allocation consequences to Delaware; the allocation problem for dual medical/adult-use conversion operators is analysis, not a Delaware official position.

1.5 Hemp

  • THC limit / measurement — production: Statute (3 Del.C. §2801(2)): delta-9 THC ≤0.3% dry weight. Regulation (3 DE Admin. Code 805 §6.0): compliance is measured on a post-decarboxylation basis — i.e. total THC (delta-9 + THCA×0.877 in substance; the 0.877 formula is expressly codified for marijuana at 4 Del.C. §1302(40)). Participant Guide extract: “If test results show a post decarboxylated delta(9)-Tetrahydrocannabinol concentration greater than 0.3 percent, the licensed producer may request a retest… Lots testing above 0.3 percent… will require destruction.” Sampling: Department sampling agent, one pre-harvest sample per lot, producer notice 30–15 days before anticipated harvest, harvest within 15 days of sampling or second sample; $350/sample (805 §§5.3, 6.1). USDA-approved state plan: Delaware plan approved 27 Jan 2020, Delaware “second state on the East Coast,” Department assumes “primary regulatory responsibility for hemp production within its jurisdiction”; FDA retains authority over manufacture of hemp products for human/animal consumption. Source: State News 28 Jan 2020 — news.delaware.gov OFFICIAL. USDA plan PDF: ams-prod.azureedge.us.
  • Licences/fees (805 §5.0): Producer: application/renewal $300/3 years + Annual Growing Site Registration $500/site/year + modification $500/site. Processor: $300/3 years + Facility Registration $1,000/facility/year + modification $1,000. Handler: $100/year. Samples $350 each. Producer applications/renewals Nov 1–Feb 1, licence valid to Dec 31 of the 3rd year; Annual Growing Site Registration due Feb 1 (805 §4.2 extracts). Criminal-history report required; material falsification = ineligibility; producer name/location records are excluded from the public record under 29 Del.C. §10002(l)(1),(2),(17)a (805 §4.1) — critical for Part 5.
  • Delta-8 / intoxicating hemp: No single Delaware statute banning delta-8 by name was opened in this run. What official law does establish: (i) THC in 4 Del.C. §1302(39) [Oct 2026] “includes delta-7… delta-8… delta-9… delta-10-tetrahydrocannabinol, an intoxicating cannabinoid, or any material… that contains their salts, isomers, and salts of isomers… regardless of the source,” with only FDA-approved dronabinol / FDA-approved reclassified THC products excepted — i.e. within the Marijuana Control Act, delta-8 etc. are THC/marijuana, not exempt hemp; (ii) §1313A protects only compliant non-beverage hemp products and only at ≤0.3% delta-9 dry weight for the “not marijuana solely because it contains cannabinoids” rule. Trade/explainer consensus that intoxicating hemp products (delta-8, delta-10, HHC, THCP, intoxicating THCA flower) are prohibited outside licensed marijuana channels is SECONDARY SOURCES ONLY — VERIFY (Marijuana Herald state guide; Burning Daily; LegalClarity — none is authority for law). Claims of a specific “HB 270 delta-8 ban” found in a commercial guide are VERIFY — NO LEGIS.DELAWARE.GOV CONFIRMATION FOUND; DO NOT CITE AS LAW.
  • THC drinks: Before 21 Oct 2026 there was no Delaware infused-beverage framework (HB 98, 2025, proposing a three-tier $1/container model, was stricken in the House 17 June 2025 [VERIFY against legis.delaware.gov BillDetail] — SECONDARY ONLY — VERIFY ON LEGIS.DELAWARE.GOV). HB 373 (2026) is now the law — see Parts 0/2/4: hemp-derived delta-9 extract only (directly extracted, not converted/synthesised from other cannabinoids, §1302(9)), 10 mg / 60 mg / 170 mg caps, package stores + retail marijuana stores + authorised microbreweries, off-premises only, warehouse-rest + state sampling/testing (§1335G), endorsement §§1335E–1335F.
  • Delaware official response to the new federal hemp definition (PL 119-37 §781 / PL 119-103 §2019): None found — UNAVAILABLE. No OMC, Department of Agriculture, or Governor statement applying the total-THC / 0.4 mg-per-container definition, the 12 Nov 2026 / 11 Dec 2026 staged dates, or the USDA lab-testing delay to Delaware was located in official sources searched. A secondary report that HB 373 contains an automatic-termination provision if federal law classifies the beverages as controlled substances is SECONDARY ONLY — VERIFY AGAINST THE ENGROSSED HB 373 TEXT; the DelCode §§1309A/1335E–1335G text read in this run contains no such sunset clause. Flag conflict as VERIFY and do not state Delaware has conformed its 0.3% delta-9 production standard to the federal total-THC product standard — it has not, on the sources read.

1.6 Packaging, labelling, advertising, delivery, zoning and buffers

  • Packaging/labelling (statute, §1331(13)): opaque, resealable, continually child-resistant (16 C.F.R. §1700.20); standard edible symbol; “not for children” label; evidence-based warning label (developing brains, machinery/driving, pregnancy/breastfeeding, prescription-drug interactions); batch number; ingredients/allergens; nutritional panel; onset-time info; serving size/count; testing results available to consumers (Oct 2026 (13)o); legible conspicuous English (translations allowed). Manufacturing: no candy/cartoon-look products (§1331(14)a). Regulation §9.0 packaging detail VERIFY FULL TEXT.
  • Advertising (§1331(15) + regulation): Commissioner must restrict advertising/marketing/signage including prohibiting mass-market campaigns with high likelihood of reaching minors. Specific rules reported from the OMC regulations (billboards prohibited; no vehicle advertising; 500-ft poster/sign buffer from schools/daycare/churches; 85%-adult audience broadcast rule; age-gating; health warning + licensee name/number; no unsubstantiated health claims / consumption depictions) are SECONDARY SUMMARIES OF THE REGULATION ONLY — VERIFY AGAINST 4 DE ADMIN. CODE 5001 TEXT before reliance.
  • Delivery: Adult-use: prohibited — retail store “may not sell… over the Internet or deliver retail marijuana or retail marijuana products to a person not physically present in the retail marijuana store’s licensed premises” (§1332(j)(3)). Infused beverages: no third-party/indirect delivery (§1309A(a)(5)a; package-store curbside pickup/delivery of its own electronic orders excepted). Medical: home delivery to qualifying patients is required of conversion retailers by attestation (§1335A(a)(7)c) and compassion-centre delivery operates under the medical programme [regulation text VERIFY].
  • Zoning/buffers (state): Commissioner spacing (pre-HB 271/§1351A overlay): refuse retail licence if existing retail establishment within 1,200 ft by accessible public road in an incorporated city/town, or 1 mile in unincorporated/rural area (discretionary grant if >0.9–<1 mile rural) (§1354(e)); Commissioner may refuse licences near a church, school, college or substance-abuse treatment facility (§1354(d)); no new licence/extension without a local certificate of compliance (zoning, permits, local licensing) (§1354(g)). County overlay (§1351A) and municipal power (§1351) — Part 3. HB 271 (signed 24 June 2026) clarifies spacing requirements apply only to retail licences; cultivation/manufacturing/testing have no additional spacing restrictions (OMC Legislative Updates, official). Whether HB 271 amended §1354(e) text as printed on DelCode as read (which still shows the general retail spacing rule) — VERIFY ENGROSSMENT.

1.7 Seed-to-sale tracking

Statute requires only “procedures for how establishments licensed under this chapter must track marijuana from seed to sale” (§1331(7)) and facility-level tracking duties (§§1332(d), 1334(c), 1335(c)). The OMC regulations (§7.0) require recording in “the seed-to-sale tracking system” a complete inventory (seeds, immature/mother/flowering plants, products), propagation/germination, inventory changes, harvest/destruction, waste destruction, transfers, diversion/discrepancies, sales, testing samples/results, pesticide applications (§7.1.2, via official Register archive extract); account must be activated before operating (§7.2.1); testing facilities must enter samples/results into the system (§11.5). State News (11 Feb 2025) confirms OMB “procured a seed-to-sale tracking system” but does not name it. Name: BioTrack, branded as the Delaware Enterprise Consolidated Cannabis Control System (DEC3S) — SECONDARY/VENDOR SOURCES ONLY (BioTrack, Dutchie) — VERIFY: NO OFFICIAL DELAWARE SOURCE NAMING BIOTRACK/DEC3S WAS OPENED IN THIS RUN. Do not present BioTrack/DEC3S as officially confirmed.

1.8 Employment and drug testing

  • Adult-use: No employment protection. 4 Del.C. §1304 (quoted §1.2) expressly preserves all employer rights on accommodation, policies and discipline. Employers may maintain zero-tolerance / drug-free workplace policies for adult-use; Delaware has no general private-employer drug-testing statute (testing types/notice: SECONDARY ONLY).
  • Medical — strong statutory protection, 16 Del.C. §4905A(a)(3):

    “Unless a failure to do so would cause the employer to lose a monetary or licensing-related benefit under federal law or federal regulations, an employer may not discriminate against a person in hiring, termination, or any term or condition of employment, or otherwise penalize a person, if the discrimination is based upon either of the following: a. The person's status as a cardholder; or b. A registered qualifying patient's positive drug test for marijuana components or metabolites, unless the patient used, possessed, or was impaired by marijuana on the premises of the place of employment or during the hours of employment.”

    Source: FindLaw/LawServer reproductions of §4905A VERIFY ON DELCODE CH. 49A. Same section protects patients/caregivers from school/landlord penalties (federal-benefit exception), treats authorised medical use as equivalent to other medication for medical care incl. organ transplants, and protects custody/visitation absent clear-and-convincing unreasonable danger. §4903A note: a registered patient is not “under the influence” solely from metabolites (via secondary extract VERIFY).
  • Industry workers: Background checks + fingerprinting for applicants, all persons performing work at/for an establishment (incl. contractors/volunteers, before beginning work), directors/officers/board members, and ≥10% owners (§1347); Commissioner-issued ID badges (§1331(10)a); retail staff ID-recognition training (§1331(10)b); labour peace agreement documentation required for active licence/renewal (§§1346(a)(3), 1332(b) etc.). HB 110 (signed 24 Apr 2025 — SECONDARY/MPP + VOTESMART; VERIFY ON LEGIS.DELAWARE.GOV) fixed the FBI fingerprint-authorisation language that had delayed launch.

PART 2

Taxes

Threshold correction: The 15% levy is not in “30 Del.C. Ch. 29A / §2901 et seq.” The levy, collection, funds and distribution are in 4 Del.C. §§1381–1389 (Subch. VIII). Title 30 matters only collaterally: Ch. 5 governs assessment/collection/appeal for the marijuana tax (§1383(d)), §368 confidentiality applies (§1383(f)), business licences are under Title 30 Ch. 21, and wholesaler/retailer gross-receipts provisions (§§2701, 2901) determine the Revenue business-licence category (Revenue FAQ). No “Ch. 29A” marijuana tax was found — VERIFY / PREMISE NOT CONFIRMED.

2.1 State marijuana tax

“A tax is imposed on the retail sale of marijuana products under this chapter in this State. This tax is not imposed on the sale of medical marijuana products under Chapter 49A of Title 16.” — §1382(a)

“The tax imposed under this section is at the rate of 15% of the retail sales price of the marijuana product.” — §1382(b)

Tax rounded up to next whole cent (§1382(c)); separately stated on invoice/receipt (§1382(d)); retailer may not discount/free-bundle a marijuana item with another item’s sale (§1382(f), in both pre-Oct and Oct 2026 versions). From 21 Oct 2026 the tax “is not imposed on the retail sale of infused beverages by a package store or a marijuana retail store” (§1382(a) Oct version) — beverages bear the per-container tax instead (below). Collection: tax imposed on the consumer, collected at point of sale, remitted by the retail licensee as taxpayer/trustee (§1383(a); Revenue FAQ: “trust fund tax,” responsible parties personally liable). Monthly report to Commissioner, Division of Revenue and DATE (§1383(b)); monthly electronic return via Delaware Taxpayer Portal (tax.delaware.gov), due 15th of following month (Revenue FAQ). Penalties (Revenue FAQ): failure-to-pay 1%/month to 25%, failure-to-file 5%/month to 50%, interest 0.5%/month. Records retained 3 years (§1384); records/civil penalty ≤$1,000. Sources: DelCode Subch. VIII; Revenue FAQ — revenue.delaware.gov OFFICIAL.

Why there is no added sales tax: Delaware imposes no general state sales tax. The 15% retail marijuana tax is therefore the entire point-of-sale tax on an adult-use purchase ($100 product = $115 at register). There is no Delaware sales/use tax layered on top, and medical purchases bear no point-of-sale tax at all. (No-sales-tax status is general Delaware law background; the operative cannabis consequence is confirmed by §§1382–1383 and the Revenue FAQ, which list no other point-of-sale levy.)

2.2 Medical vs adult-use

Medical marijuana products under Ch. 49A: exempt from the 15% tax (§1382(a)). Revenue FAQ: “only consumers who provide a valid medical marijuana registry identification card at the time of purchase are exempt… No [other exemptions].” Non-marijuana items sold by a retailer are not subject to the retail marijuana tax (Revenue FAQ). Retailers must keep records of tax-exempt cardholder sales (card number, date, category, product, price, units, total — OMC Reg. §13.8 extract via official Register archive).

2.3 Gross receipts / business licence / income tax

  • Gross receipts: “Marijuana products are exempt from retail gross receipts tax” (Division of Revenue Retail Marijuana Tax slides, Dec 2024 — revenuefiles.delaware.gov OFFICIAL). Establishments still need a Delaware business licence (Title 30 Ch. 21) before OMC licensure, mapped: Testing → General Services; Cultivation → Wholesale; Manufacturing → Food Processor / Manufacturers (per §2701(2), §2901(21)a.2); Retailer → Retail Sales (Revenue FAQ). Converted businesses became subject to applicable licensing, gross-receipts and business income tax requirements as of conversion (Revenue slides, re HB 408/425) — i.e. the retail-GRT exemption is for marijuana products, not a blanket exemption of the business from all GRT/income tax on other activity VERIFY ENTITY-LEVEL APPLICATION.
  • State preemption of local business taxes: §1383(e):

    “The fees, charges, and taxes imposed by the State under this chapter shall be in lieu of all county and municipal license fees and taxes upon the business of selling, growing, and manufacturing marijuana as such. Provided, however, general occupational licenses fees and general taxes imposed uniformly on everyone within the class are not preempted.”

    County/municipal cannabis-specific taxes: none may be imposed on the marijuana business as such; only uniform general fees/taxes survive. Wilmington City Council in May 2025 adopted a resolution urging the General Assembly to amend the Code to enable a city marijuana excise — confirming no local excise power currently exists (City Council PDF — wilmingtoncitycouncil.com, official municipal). Gov. Meyer’s SB 75 veto message proposed directing ~⅓ of state tax to host county/municipality — a proposal, not law (SECONDARY — SPOTLIGHT DELAWARE).

2.4 IRC §280E and Delaware decoupling (under the split baseline)

Delaware decouples at state level. Revenue FAQ: “Can I claim the cost of doing business as a deduction against Delaware Personal or Corporate Income Taxes? Yes. While the IRS prevents federal income taxpayers involved in the marijuana business from deducting ordinary and necessary business expenses, Delaware tax forms will allow these deductions separately. In the event federal laws or regulations change such that the deduction… is allowed by the IRS, the existing Delaware deduction will be voided to prevent double deductions.” Revenue slides: deduction = “ordinary and necessary business expenses… disallowed as a deduction for federal purposes pursuant to §280E,” Delaware-only and “only valid if the same deduction has not already occurred on the federal return.” Statutory home of the deduction in Title 30 VERIFY SECTION NUMBER — NOT OPENED; DO NOT CITE A SECTION.

Meaning under the split baseline: Federally, state-licensed medical activity (Schedule III) is no longer 280E-barred, while adult-use activity (Schedule I) remains fully 280E-barred. Delaware’s deduction therefore now does different work for the two sides of a dual operator: for the medical side, the federal deduction is (per baseline, guidance forthcoming) available federally, so the Delaware “void if federally allowed / no double deduction” rule switches the state deduction off to the extent the expense is taken federally; for the adult-use side, 280E still bars the federal deduction, so the Delaware deduction remains the only relief (rent, payroll, marketing etc. deductible for Delaware personal/corporate income tax, not federally; COGS deductible at both levels regardless). Mixed medical/adult-use conversion operators must allocate expenses between the two activities — Delaware has published no allocation guidance found in this run UNAVAILABLE. Effective date of Delaware decoupling: secondary sources say spring 2023 as part of the legalisation wave VERIFY STATUTORY EFFECTIVE DATE.

2.5 Infused-beverage tax (from 1 Feb 2027)

$0.50 per single-serving container; $8.50 per 750 mL bottle (OMC Infused Beverages page, official). OMC Legislative Updates summarises only “a $0.50 per-container tax” — the $8.50 bottle rate comes from the OMC beverage page; Title 30 codification VERIFY. Beverages are exempt from the 15% retail marijuana tax (§1382 Oct version).

2.6 Revenue figures and statutory distribution

Distribution (§§1381, 1386, 1387): All marijuana tax + fees → Marijuana Regulation Fund (State Treasurer) (§1386(a)(1)). Monthly, 7% of total marijuana tax received in the preceding month → Justice Reinvestment Fund (§1386(a)(2)). Regulation Fund appropriated to: (1) Commissioner/DATE admin; (2) Finance, DHSS, Agriculture (and, Oct 2026 version, Justice) and other regulating agencies on an approved expenditure plan; (3) Criminal Justice Council admin of the Justice Reinvestment Fund; unencumbered Regulation Fund balance capped at $5,000,000 at fiscal year-end, remainder → General Fund (§1387(a)). Justice Reinvestment Fund → Criminal Justice Council grants/contracts for restorative justice/jail diversion/workforce development/technical assistance in DIAs; addressing causes of crime, reducing drug arrests and prison population; civil-rights restoration/expungement technology; supporting social equity applicants with select business expenses; reentry services (§1387(b)); no reversion.

Collections (official): First weekend est. tax $93,750 (Part 0). Aug–Dec 2025 tax revenue $3,112,599 — figure published by MPP citing state data; treat as SECONDARY REPORTING OF OFFICIAL DATA — VERIFY AGAINST DEPT. OF FINANCE TRANSPARENCY PROJECT. Revenue FAQ directs all tax-collection data to “The Transparency Project: Delaware” (Dept. of Finance) — no collection total was read from that dashboard in this run UNAVAILABLE — SEE PART 5. First-12-months tax (~$8.0m on $53.4m sales; alternatively $8.8m on $58.5m) is SECONDARY SOURCES ONLY — VERIFY (Part 6 conflict). Application-fee revenue: >$4m from 1,269 applications (MPP summary of OMC figures — secondary). Conversion-fee revenue: $4m (official, Part 0), earmarked only for social-equity financial assistance (§1339(b)).

PART 3

Local rules

3.1 County vs municipal — stated clearly

Both, asymmetrically. Delaware has exactly 3 counties — New Castle, Kent, Sussex. Municipalities (incorporated cities/towns) govern establishments inside their boundaries and may prohibit them outright. Counties govern unincorporated areas only (land outside any incorporated municipality); county law does not displace a town’s own ordinance inside that town, and a town ban does not govern the unincorporated county land outside it. After SB 75, counties may not prohibit establishments in the ways §1351A forbids (below) — county power is now “limit/regulate within state parameters,” while the municipal prohibition power in §1351(a) was not removed by the §1351A text read. Applicants also need a local certificate of compliance (§1354(g)) from “the appropriate political subdivision.”

3.2 Opt-in/opt-out, moratoriums, caps — §§1351 / 1351A verified

Delaware is opt-out, not opt-in: establishments are lawful statewide unless a municipality prohibits/regulates them; no municipality must affirmatively opt in.

§1351 Local control; municipalities:

“(a) A municipality may prohibit the operation of marijuana establishments through the enactment of an ordinance or through an initiated or referred measure. (b) A municipality may enact ordinances or regulations that do not conflict with this chapter… governing the time, place, manner, and number of marijuana establishment operations. A municipality may establish civil penalties…” — §1351 (last amended 85 Del. Laws, c. 333, §2 — i.e. SB 75)

§1351A Local control; counties (85 Del. Laws, c. 333, §3; c. 461, §8):

“(a)(1) A county may limit the operation of marijuana establishments by enacting ordinances or regulations that do not conflict with this chapter… (2) An ordinance or regulation governing the time or manner… must allow a retail marijuana store to be open no less than… Monday through Saturday, between 9 a.m. and 9 p.m… Sunday, between noon and 8 p.m. (3)… this section preempts and supersedes all existing and future county ordinances or regulations regarding the operation of marijuana establishments…”

“(b) If a compassion center is granted a conversion license… a county must allow the retail marijuana store to operate as a nonconforming use… however a county may not deny a building permit… if the improvements comply with the physical requirements for properties in that zoning category.”

“(c) In an area that is zoned for agricultural or industrial use, a county may not prohibit a marijuana cultivation facility that is indoors and fully enclosed.”

“(d) In an area that is zoned for commercial or industrial use, a county may not prohibit the operation of a retail marijuana store except as follows: (1) Within a ½ mile of another retail marijuana store. (2) Within 500 feet of any of the following: a. A school or institution of higher education. b. A child care facility licensed under Chapter 30A of Title 14. c. A residential treatment facility… d. A park or library.”

“(e) A marijuana establishment that is operating lawfully may continue to operate as a nonconforming use… after [a county zoning change or a listed entity is established]. (f) A county may establish civil penalties…”

Source: delcode.delaware.gov/title4/c013/sc04 OFFICIAL. Effect: pre-SB 75 county regimes exceeding these parameters (notably Sussex’s 3-mile buffers) are preempted/superseded as a matter of state law. Whether each county code has been conformed is a separate factual question — New Castle County Council in Oct 2026 voted not to align its ordinance, asserting continued authority (Delaware Online, secondary — see §3.5). Moratoriums/review dates: no state moratorium; individual municipal moratoriums UNAVAILABLE — NO OFFICIAL STATEWIDE LIST, §3.3. Local caps: municipalities may regulate “number” (§1351(b)); counties may “limit” (§1351A(a)(1)) subject to §1351A(c)–(d) prohibitions-on-prohibition. No local licence category exists (state licence + local certificate of compliance + uniform local business licence only, §1383(e)).

3.3 Official list of jurisdictions that allow/prohibit — cannot be fully retrieved

No official statewide county/municipal allow/prohibit list was found on OMC, DelCode, or data.delaware.gov in this run — UNAVAILABLE. The OMC site as read contains no such list; the exact place to check is https://omc.delaware.gov/ (Licensing/Local resources) — accessed 24 Oct 2026, no list retrieved.

What can be stated, with sources:

  • Sussex County municipalities prohibiting (as of early 2024, county-meeting record): Rehoboth Beach, Dewey Beach, Bethany Beach, Millville, Seaford*, Ocean View, Fenwick Island, Millsboro, Dagsboro, Delmar had prohibited facilities (Sussex County Ord. 3016, adopted 14 May 2024, official county — Middletown is in New Castle County, not Sussex County, and is removed from this Sussex list; the remaining towns are secondary-only, each town’s code unchecked — sussexcountyde.civicweb.net OFFICIAL COUNTY). *Seaford later reversed for its existing operator: amended late Aug 2025 to allow Fresh Delaware (Seaford) recreational sales from 4 Oct 2025 — SECONDARY (SPOTLIGHT DELAWARE) — VERIFY IN SEAFORD CODE.
  • Sussex allowing (secondary, verify in town codes): Georgetown (first in Sussex to allow retail, summer 2024, Highway Commercial only, 1,000-ft sensitive-use buffer), Frankford (no ordinance enacted → state law applies; The Farm opened there), Seaford (as above). Spotlight Delaware (Oct 2025): “All of the 25 towns in Sussex County have outlawed retail marijuana entirely, except for Seaford, Georgetown and Frankford.” — SECONDARY SOURCES ONLY — VERIFY TOWN-BY-TOWN.
  • Statewide scale: “over a third of Delaware’s 57 municipalities have created bans on various types of marijuana establishments” (Spotlight Delaware, Sept 2025, secondary); “about one-third of municipalities still prohibit” (secondary summary). Do not present any town list as complete or current without checking each municipal code.
  • Verified individual municipal actions (official municipal sources): Town of Hartly (Kent Co.) Ordinance #13-02-2025-0001, adopted 10 Apr 2025, “prohibits marijuana cultivation facilities…, marijuana product manufacturing facilities, marijuana testing facilities, and/or retail marijuana stores” in Hartly, with penalties — hartly.delaware.gov OFFICIAL MUNICIPAL. Town of Cheswold (Kent Co.) Ordinance 08-16-24-074 “Marijuana-Based Businesses” (16 Aug 2024) finally adopted 2 December 2024 (16 Aug 2024 is the ordinance number/first-reading date); it permits marijuana establishments in the C-2 (Highway Commercial), AG (Agriculture) and I-1 (Light Industrial) districts, subject to conditional-use approval — Cheswold is not a banning town — cheswold.delaware.gov/ordinances OFFICIAL MUNICIPAL.
  • Other towns reported (secondary only): Middletown ban; Milford ban on recreational retail (2025, Bisnow); Newark DE allowing all business types with 300-ft K-12 buffer except a Main-Street-only retail restriction reported in an earlier summary — conflicting secondary accounts for Newark — VERIFY in Newark DE code (not Newark, NJ); Dover DE allowing all types, buffer reduced 1 mile → 500 ft after mayoral veto override (Bisnow, secondary — verify in Dover DE code, not Dover NH); Wilmington considering downtown-focused zoning / residential buffers and (separately) urging a local excise (Wilmington City Council, official for the excise resolution; zoning outcome VERIFY). Smyrna allowing retail with 1,200-ft store-to-store spacing (secondary).

3.4 Local licence/permit fees/requirements

No jurisdiction-specific cannabis licence fee was verified from an official county/municipal source in this run — UNAVAILABLE. State law bars cannabis-specific local licence fees/taxes (§1383(e)); lawful local charges are limited to (i) the §1354(g) certificate-of-compliance process (zoning/building/fire permits at generally applicable rates), (ii) uniform general occupational/business licence fees and taxes, and (iii) civil penalties under §1351(b)/§1351A(f). Any per-town permit fee schedule must be taken from that town/county code — flagged for Run 2 verification for Georgetown, Seaford, Dover, Smyrna, Newark, Wilmington, and the three counties.

PART 4

Licence types

All licences are biennial (2-year) unless stated. Application fees: Open $5,000 (§1331(5)); Social equity $1,000 (§1337(a)(2)); Microbusiness $3,000 (§1341(a)(2)) — all nonrefundable, Commissioner may adjust for inflation. Licence/renewal fees: social equity and microbusiness pay 40% of the applicable open licence fee — note the statute says “at a discounted rate of 40% of the applicable open license” (§§1337(b), 1341(b)), i.e. they pay 40% (= 60% reduction). Secondary sources that say “40% off” are wrong on the statutory wording — flagged. Renewal requires fee + labour peace agreement documentation (+ social-equity/micro criteria confirmation).

Statutory lottery pool — 125 licences (§1343)

Conversion licences for existing compassion centres are additional to the 125. Source: 4 Del.C. §1343; OMC (State of Delaware News). Accessed 24 Oct 2026.

  • Canopy ceiling: No cultivation licence for indoor >12,500 sq ft or outdoor >7.5 acres unless Commissioner creates additional tiers (§1334(f)); Commissioner may create tiers any time after 1 Aug 2025 if demand requires (§1334(h)); licensee may request +1 tier per renewal with updated diversion plan (§1334(g)). Canopy = flowering-plant area, horizontal measure incl. each vertical tier/shelf (≤24 ft), excl. harvest/dry/cure/package/label/storage areas (§1334(b)(2)).
  • Ownership cap: No person may own/operate more than 1 establishment of the same licence type in a single county (§1344(a)); different types in one county allowed. Licences are county-assigned — OMC clarified 22 Sept 2025 that county-based assignments have not been removed (State News headline/extract; full text VERIFY).
  • Applications open/capped? The §1343 pools are the statutory issuance schedule (cultivation from 1 Nov 2024, manufacturing from 1 Dec 2024, retail/testing from 1 Mar 2025), expressly “provided a sufficient number of qualified applicants exist,” with locality-opposition/lack-of-applicants as a defence (§1343(f)). After 1 Aug 2025 the Commissioner may accept applications for any type and issue in excess of the §1343 numbers if additional facilities are needed or a type is under-filled (§1343(g)). Current status: OMC is no longer accepting applications for the lottery round (secondary summary of OMC position VERIFY CURRENT OMC LICENSING PAGE); no upcoming lottery window was announced in official sources read — UNAVAILABLE. Do not present a “2026 second round” as official: one secondary summary claims a Q2 2026 retail round — SECONDARY ONLY, NO OMC CONFIRMATION FOUND VERIFY.
  • Lotteries held: Applications accepted 19 Aug–30 Sept 2024 (statute said Commissioner may begin 1 Sept 2024, §1343(a) — OMC opened early). OMC official figures: 1,271 received, 1,256 proceeded to lottery (11 Feb 2025 release) vs 1,269 paid applications (1 July 2025 release) — conflict noted; most authoritative = OMC Feb 2025 detailed release (1,271/1,256) for the lottery denominator, OMC July 2025 release (1,269 paid) for paid count; the 2-application difference is unexplained VERIFY. Lotteries: 24 Oct 2024 and 19 Dec 2024 (second because retail demand: 529 applications for 15 retail permits — Feb release wording; read as the open-retail pool). 125 licences awarded across the October and December 2024 lotteries (OMC/State News, multiple releases). Secondary counts of 124 (MPP) are not used — official OMC figure 125 controls. Selected applicants’ background documentation was due 10 Feb 2025; conditional licence = 18 months to secure premises, complete requirements, get premises approval and demonstrate readiness (§1302(4); §1345 — Commissioner may extend for good-faith efforts, otherwise must rescind). Active licence requires: background-check fees paid, financial/ownership docs approved, labour peace agreement attestation, local zoning/fire/building compliance, any additional Commissioner requirements (§1346).
  • Social equity programme: Eligibility (§1336): ≥51% ownership/control by individuals who (1) resided ≥5 of preceding 15 years in a DIA, or (2) were convicted/adjudicated delinquent of a Delaware marijuana-related offence before 23 Apr 2023 (except delivery to a minor or Tier 3 quantity under 16 Del.C. §4751C), or have a parent/legal guardian/child/spouse/dependent so convicted. Technical assistance programme required (§1338, may partner with Division of Small Business). Financial assistance (§1339): Commissioner must investigate public/private assistance incl. Justice Reinvestment Fund; “All funds derived from the issuance of conversion licenses shall only be used as sources of financial assistance for social equity applicants issued a conditional license.” In operation: Social Equity Financial Assistance (SEFA) grants, seeded by the $4m conversion fees (State News: grant applications accessible Mar 2025, procedures mailed to eligible applicants; Sept 2025: state “will soon begin distributing SEFA Grants”). The “$4.1m + $2m” SEFA figure is rejected — official OMC funding is $4m; the Sept 2025 SEFA release states no dollar total and no per-licence amounts. No Delaware social-equity loan programme was verified — grants + technical assistance + fee discounts only on official sources LOAN PROGRAMME UNAVAILABLE. DIA map / Social Equity Eligibility Validation Form exist on OMC site (secondary description VERIFY URLS IN PART 5).
  • Estimated startup costs: No official estimate exists — UNAVAILABLE. Non-official operator/consultant estimates were not relied on for law/fees above; any startup figure in Run 2 must be marked “non-official estimate.” Official cost floor = application fee + biennial licence fee + conversion fee (if applicable) + background-check/badge/local permit costs, excl. premises, build-out, security, insurance, staffing.

Regulator info pages: OMC — omc.delaware.gov; Adult Use — omc.delaware.gov/adult-use; Dispensaries — omc.delaware.gov/dispensaries; Infused Beverages — omc.delaware.gov/infused-beverages (all accessed 24 Oct 2026, official). Public contact on OMC pages: 302-244-3662 (official public contact).

6.2 Banks / credit unions

No cannabis-friendly bank or credit union serving Delaware licensees is named in any official Delaware source read — UNAVAILABLE. What is official/established: HB 355 (2024) gives state-law protection to banks, credit unions, armoured-car and accounting providers serving licensed businesses (Part 1.9; signed — secondary date verification pending); OMC application rules require a FinCEN/Bank Secrecy Act compliance plan description, which may be shown by bank/credit-union letters certifying awareness of the applicant’s marijuana business activity, without disqualification if not submitted (OMC Reg. §4.2.1.1, official Register archive). Retail marijuana tax cannot be paid in cash — electronic payment only (Revenue FAQ). Per baseline, no federal banking law has been enacted; naming specific institutions from commercial “cannabis banking” lists would be non-official and is not done here.
